```text
1	\section{Proofs of Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip} and Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone}}
2	\label{app:proof}
3	
4	We provide algebraic proofs of sign preservation for the confounding and collider topologies (Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip}(a)--(b)), an explicit counterexample for exposure (Proposition~\ref{prop:signflip}(c)), numerical verification for mediation, M-bias, and front-door (Numerical Verification~1), and the monotonicity proof for confounding (Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone}).
5	Throughout, $A\in\{0,1,\dots,K{-}1\}$ with prior $p_k=P(A{=}k)$, and $C$ is a $K{\times}K$ column-stochastic confusion matrix with $C_{jk}=P(A^*{=}j\mid A{=}k)$.
6	We use category~0 as the reference and let $D_j=\mathbf{1}(A{=}j)$, $D_j^*=\mathbf{1}(A^*{=}j)$ for $j=1,\dots,K{-}1$.
7	
8	\subsection{Notation and Key Identities}
9	\label{app:notation}
10	
11	Under non-differential misclassification, the misclassified moments factor linearly through $C$.
12	Define the population vectors:
13	\begin{align}
14	  q_j &= P(A^*{=}j) = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,p_k, \label{eq:q}\\
15	  \mathrm{ct}_j &= \mathbb{E}[T\cdot D_j^*] = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,\mathbb{E}[T\cdot D_k], \label{eq:ct}\\
16	  \mathrm{cy}_j &= \mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_j^*] = \textstyle\sum_k C_{jk}\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_k]. \label{eq:cy}
17	\end{align}
18	Write $q = Cp$, $\mathrm{ct} = C\,\mathbb{E}[T\cdot D]$, and $\mathrm{cy} = C\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D]$, where $D=(D_0,\dots,D_{K-1})^\top$.
19	
20	\subsection{Frisch--Waugh--Lovell Decomposition}
21	\label{app:fwl}
22	
23	For topologies where $T$ is directly observed (confounding, mediation, collider, M-bias, front-door), the estimator regresses $Y$ on $Z^*=(1,T,D_1^*,\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.
24	By the Frisch--Waugh--Lovell (FWL) theorem, the coefficient on $T$ satisfies:
25	\begin{equation}
26	  \operatorname{plim}\hat{\tau}(C)
27	  = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\tilde{Y}]}{\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]},
28	  \label{eq:fwl}
29	\end{equation}
30	where $\tilde{T}=T-\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]$ and $\tilde{Y}=Y-\mathbb{E}[Y\mid A^*]$ are residuals from projecting onto $(1,D_1^*,\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.
31	
32	\begin{lemma}[Bias decomposition]
33	\label{lem:bias_decomp}
34	Suppose $Y = \tau T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y$ with $\varepsilon_Y \perp (T, A, A^*)$.
35	Then
36	\begin{equation}
37	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \tau + \frac{R(C)}{D(C)},
38	  \label{eq:bias_decomp}
39	\end{equation}
40	where
41	\begin{align}
42	  R(C) &= \mathbb{E}\bigl[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*)\bigr], \label{eq:R}\\
43	  D(C) &= \mathbb{E}\bigl[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)\bigr] > 0. \label{eq:D}
44	\end{align}
45	\end{lemma}
46	
47	\begin{proof}
48	Expand $\tilde{Y}$ using $Y=\tau T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y$:
49	\[
50	  \tilde{Y} = \tau\,\tilde{T} + \bigl(f(A) - \mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr) + \varepsilon_Y.
51	\]
52	Since $\tilde{T}$ is measurable w.r.t.\ $(T,A^*)$ and $\varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A,A^*)$:
53	\[
54	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,\tilde{Y}]
55	  = \tau\,\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]
56	  + \mathbb{E}\bigl[\tilde{T}\,\bigl(f(A)-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)\bigr].
57	\]
58	The numerator residual is $R(C) = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}(f(A)-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*])]$, and the denominator is $D(C) = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]$.
59	
60	By the law of total covariance:
61	\[
62	  R(C) = \sum_{j=0}^{K-1} q_j\,\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*{=}j)
63	  = \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)].
64	\]
65	For $D(C)>0$: decompose $T = g(A) + \varepsilon_T$ where $g(A)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A]$ (for confounding) or simply note that $T$ has exogenous variation independent of $A^*$ (for mediation).
66	In both cases, $\tilde{T}=\bigl(g(A)-\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]\bigr)+\varepsilon_T'$, where $\varepsilon_T'$ is the component of $T$ independent of $(A,A^*)$.
67	Hence $D(C)\geq\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_T')>0$.
68	\end{proof}
69	
70	\subsection{Confounding Topology ($A$ = Confounder)}
71	\label{app:confounding}
72	
73	\paragraph{Data-generating process.}
74	$A\to T$, $A\to Y$, $T\to Y$.
75	Using dummy coding with category 0 as reference:
76	\begin{align*}
77	  T &= \sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\alpha_j D_j + \varepsilon_T,\quad
78	  \varepsilon_T\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_T^2),\;\varepsilon_T\perp A,\\
79	  Y &= \tau\, T + \sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\beta_j^{(A)} D_j + \varepsilon_Y,\quad
80	  \varepsilon_Y\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1),\;\varepsilon_Y\perp(T,A).
81	\end{align*}
82	Define $g(k)=\sum_{j}\alpha_j\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$ and $f(k)=\sum_{j}\beta_j^{(A)}\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$, so $g(0)=f(0)=0$.
83	
84	\paragraph{Closed-form plim.}
85	Using the FWL decomposition, the coefficient on $T$ is:
86	\begin{equation}
87	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C)
88	  = \frac{\mu_{TY} - \sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\frac{\mathrm{ct}_j\,\mathrm{cy}_j}{q_j}}
89	  {s_T - \sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\frac{\mathrm{ct}_j^2}{q_j}},
90	  \label{eq:plim_conf}
91	\end{equation}
92	where $s_T=\mathbb{E}[T^2]$, $\mu_{TY}=\mathbb{E}[TY]$, and $\mathrm{ct}_j$, $\mathrm{cy}_j$, $q_j$ are defined in~\eqref{eq:q}--\eqref{eq:cy}.
93	
94	\begin{proof}[Derivation]
95	By FWL, $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}Y]/\mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]$ where $\tilde{T}=T-\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]$.
96	Since $\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*{=}j]=\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j$:
97	\begin{align*}
98	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}^2]
99	  &= \mathbb{E}[T^2] - \sum_j q_j\bigl(\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\bigr)^2
100	  = s_T - \sum_j \mathrm{ct}_j^2/q_j,\\
101	  \mathbb{E}[\tilde{T}\,Y]
102	  &= \mathbb{E}[TY] - \sum_j \bigl(\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\bigr)\,\mathbb{E}[Y\cdot D_j^*]
103	  = \mu_{TY} - \sum_j \mathrm{ct}_j\,\mathrm{cy}_j/q_j.
104	\end{align*}
105	\end{proof}
106	
107	\paragraph{Verification at $C=I$.}
108	When $C=I$: $q_j=p_j$, $\mathrm{ct}_j=\mathbb{E}[T D_j]=\alpha_j p_j$, $\mathrm{cy}_j=\mathbb{E}[Y D_j]=p_j(\tau\alpha_j+\beta_j^{(A)})$.
109	Direct substitution yields numerator $= \tau\sigma_T^2$ and denominator $= \sigma_T^2$, giving $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \tau$.
110	
111	\paragraph{Bias structure.}
112	By Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}:
113	\begin{equation}
114	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C) = \tau + B(C),\quad
115	  B(C) = \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\,f(A)\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.
116	  \label{eq:bias_conf}
117	\end{equation}
118	By the law of total covariance:
119	\begin{equation}
120	  \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\mid A^*)]
121	  = \operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))
122	  - \operatorname{Cov}\bigl(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*],\;\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid A^*]\bigr).
123	  \label{eq:lotc}
124	\end{equation}
125	
126	At $C=I$: $\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*]=g(A)$, so the between-group covariance equals $\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)$ and $B(I)=0$.
127	
128	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ (uniform, $A^*\perp A$): $\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*]$ is constant, so the between-group covariance is zero and
129	\begin{equation}
130	  B_{\mathrm{OV}} \;\equiv\; B\bigl(\tfrac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top\bigr)
131	  = \frac{\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\,f(A))}{\operatorname{Var}(T)},
132	  \label{eq:bov}
133	\end{equation}
134	which equals the classical omitted-variable bias (no control for $A$).
135	
136	\paragraph{Sign preservation condition.}
137	Since $D(C)>0$, sign$(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau) = $ sign$(\tau\cdot D(C) + R(C))$.
138	For sign preservation, it suffices that $R(C)$ does not overwhelm $\tau\cdot D(C)$.
139	
140	\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- proportional case]
141	\label{prop:conf_prop}
142	If $f(A) = \lambda\,g(A)$ for some constant $\lambda$ (i.e., the confounder effects on $T$ and $Y$ are proportional), then
143	\begin{equation}
144	  B(C) = \bigl(1-\xi(C)\bigr)\,B_{\mathrm{OV}},
145	  \label{eq:convex}
146	\end{equation}
147	where
148	\[
149	  \xi(C) = \frac{\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])\;\sigma_T^2}
150	  {\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\bigl(\operatorname{Var}(g(A))-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*])+\sigma_T^2\bigr)}
151	  \;\in\;[0,1].
152	\]
153	Consequently, $B(C)$ lies between $0$ and $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$, and $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ whenever $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau+B_{\mathrm{OV}})$.
154	\end{proposition}
155	
156	\begin{proof}
157	When $f=\lambda g$: the between-group covariance in~\eqref{eq:lotc} becomes $\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])$, and $\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)=\lambda\,\operatorname{Var}(g)$.
158	So $R(C)=\lambda\bigl(\operatorname{Var}(g)-\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])\bigr)=\lambda\,V_W$, where $V_W=\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B$ with $V_B=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*])$.
159	Similarly, $D(C)=V_W+\sigma_T^2$.
160	Hence
161	\[
162	  B(C) = \frac{\lambda\,V_W}{V_W+\sigma_T^2},\qquad
163	  B_{\mathrm{OV}} = \frac{\lambda\,V}{V+\sigma_T^2},
164	\]
165	where $V=\operatorname{Var}(g)=V_B+V_W$.
166	The ratio
167	\[
168	  \frac{B(C)}{B_{\mathrm{OV}}} = \frac{V_W(V+\sigma_T^2)}{V(V_W+\sigma_T^2)} = 1 - \xi(C)
169	\]
170	is a decreasing function of $V_B\in[0,V]$.
171	At $V_B=0$ (no classification, $C$ uniform): $\xi=0$, $B=B_{\mathrm{OV}}$.
172	At $V_B=V$ (perfect classification, $C=I$): $\xi=1$, $B=0$.
173	Since $0\leq V_B\leq V$, we have $\xi\in[0,1]$ and $B(C)\in[0,B_{\mathrm{OV}}]$ (with the sign of $B_{\mathrm{OV}}$).
174	\end{proof}
175	
176	\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- general case]
177	\label{prop:conf_gen}
178	For general $g$ and $f$ (not necessarily proportional), define the worst-case bias bound
179	\begin{equation}
180	  \bar{B} \;=\; \frac{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\,\operatorname{Var}(f(A))}}{\sigma_T^2}.
181	  \label{eq:bbar}
182	\end{equation}
183	If $|\tau| > \bar{B}$, then $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ for \emph{all} column-stochastic $C$.
184	\end{proposition}
185	
186	\begin{proof}
187	We need $\operatorname{sign}(\tau\cdot D(C)+R(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$, which holds if $|R(C)|<|\tau|\cdot D(C)$.
188	Since $D(C)\geq\sigma_T^2$ (shown in Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}), it suffices to show $|R(C)|<|\tau|\,\sigma_T^2$.
189	
190	\emph{Step 1: Bound $|R(C)|$.}
191	$R(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A)\mid A^*)]$.
192	By the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality applied to each within-group covariance:
193	\[
194	  |\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\mid A^*{=}j)|
195	  \leq \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*{=}j)\,\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*{=}j)}.
196	\]
197	Taking expectations and applying Cauchy--Schwarz again:
198	\begin{align*}
199	  |R(C)|
200	  &\leq \mathbb{E}\bigl[\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*)\,\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*)}\bigr]\\
201	  &\leq \sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*)]\;\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*)]}\\
202	  &\leq \sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\;\operatorname{Var}(f(A))},
203	\end{align*}
204	where the last step uses $\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(X\mid Y)]\leq\operatorname{Var}(X)$ (law of total variance).
205	
206	\emph{Step 2: Conclude.}
207	The bias satisfies
208	\[
209	  |B(C)| = \frac{|R(C)|}{D(C)} \leq \frac{\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}(g)\,\operatorname{Var}(f)}}{\sigma_T^2} = \bar{B}.
210	\]
211	When $|\tau|>\bar{B}$, the bias cannot cancel $\tau$, so $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau)$ for all $C$.
212	\end{proof}
213	
214	\paragraph{Relationship to the omitted-variable condition.}
215	A weaker (necessary) condition for sign preservation is $\operatorname{sign}(\tau)=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{OV}})$ where $\tau_{\mathrm{OV}}=\tau+\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\operatorname{Var}(T)$.
216	If this fails, then $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ (uniform noise) already flips the sign.
217	The rigorous sufficient condition $|\tau|>\bar{B}$ implies the weaker condition, since $|B_{\mathrm{OV}}|=|\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)|/\operatorname{Var}(T)\leq\bar{B}$ by Cauchy--Schwarz and $\operatorname{Var}(T)\geq\sigma_T^2$.
218	
219	\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}
220	With $\tau{=}2.0$, $\boldsymbol{\alpha}{=}(1.5,{-}1.0)$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}^{(A)}{=}(1.0,{-}0.5)$, $\sigma_T{=}1.0$, $\mathbf{p}{=}(0.40,0.35,0.25)$:
221	\begin{align*}
222	  \operatorname{Var}(g) &= \sum_k p_k\alpha_k^2 - \bigl(\textstyle\sum_k p_k\alpha_k\bigr)^2 = 0.962,\\
223	  \operatorname{Var}(f) &= \sum_k p_k(\beta_k^{(A)})^2 - \bigl(\textstyle\sum_k p_k\beta_k^{(A)}\bigr)^2 = 0.362,\\
224	  \bar{B} &= \sqrt{0.962\times 0.362}\;/\;1.0^2 = 0.590.
225	\end{align*}
226	Since $|\tau|{=}2.0 > \bar{B}{=}0.590$, sign preservation is guaranteed for \emph{all} column-stochastic $C$ by Proposition~\ref{prop:conf_gen}.
227	Additionally, $B_{\mathrm{OV}}=\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.588/1.962={+}0.300$, giving $\tau_{\mathrm{OV}}{=}2.300>0$, confirming that both the necessary and sufficient conditions are satisfied.
228	
229	\paragraph{Extension to $K > 3$.}
230	For $K{>}3$, treatment effect coefficients are drawn from a fixed pool with alternating signs and decreasing magnitudes: $\boldsymbol{\beta} = (0, 1.0, {-}0.5, 0.7, {-}0.3, 0.5, {-}0.2, 0.4, {-}0.15, 0.3, \ldots)$, truncated to $K$ entries. The category distribution uses $p_k \propto 1/(k{+}1)$, normalized to sum to 1. Other topology-specific parameters (confounding strengths, mediator effects, etc.) follow analogous alternating-sign pools. For $K{=}10$, $\boldsymbol{\beta} = (0, 1.0, {-}0.5, 0.7, {-}0.3, 0.5, {-}0.2, 0.4, {-}0.15, 0.3)$.
231	
232	\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone} (Confounding Monotonicity)}
233	\label{app:monotonicity_proof}
234	
235	Under the interpolation $C(\delta)=(1{-}\delta)I+\delta C_0$, we prove $|B(C(\delta))|$ is non-decreasing in $\delta$ for proportional effects ($f=\lambda g$) and provide numerical verification for the general case.
236	The proof proceeds by direct algebraic analysis of $V_B(\delta)=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*_\delta])$ as a rational function of $\delta$, establishing convexity and bounding the derivative at both endpoints.
237	
238	\paragraph{Proportional case ($f=\lambda g$).}
239	From Proposition~\ref{prop:conf_prop}, $B(C)=\lambda V_W/(V_W+\sigma_T^2)$ where $V_W=\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B$ and $V_B(\delta)=\operatorname{Var}(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*_\delta])$.
240	
241	\emph{Step 1: Convexity of $V_B(\delta)$.}
242	Define $N_j(\delta)=\sum_k g_k\,C(\delta)_{jk}\,p_k$ and $q_j(\delta)=\sum_k C(\delta)_{jk}\,p_k$.
243	Both are affine in $\delta$, with $q_j(\delta)>0$.
244	Then $V_B(\delta)=\sum_j N_j(\delta)^2/q_j(\delta) - \bar{g}^2$.
245	Each term $N_j^2/q_j$ is the composition of the perspective function $(x,t)\mapsto x^2/t$ (convex for $t>0$) with the affine map $\delta\mapsto(N_j(\delta),\,q_j(\delta))$, hence convex in $\delta$.
246	A sum of convex functions is convex, so $V_B(\delta)$ is convex on $[0,\delta_{\max}]$.
247	
248	\emph{Step 2: Derivative formula.}
249	Let $\mu_j(\delta)=N_j(\delta)/q_j(\delta)=\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*{=}j]$.
250	Then
251	\begin{equation}
252	\frac{dV_B}{d\delta}
253	= -\sum_{j,k}\bigl[(C_0)_{jk}-\mathbf{1}_{j=k}\bigr]\,p_k\,(g_k-\mu_j(\delta))^2.
254	\label{eq:dVB}
255	\end{equation}
256	\emph{Derivation.}
257	$\frac{d}{d\delta}\frac{N_j^2}{q_j}=2\mu_j\dot{N}_j-\mu_j^2\dot{q}_j$
258	where $\dot{N}_j=m_j^0-g_jp_j$ and $\dot{q}_j=q_j^0-p_j$ (with $m_j^0=\sum_k g_k(C_0)_{jk}p_k$, $q_j^0=\sum_k(C_0)_{jk}p_k$).
259	Substituting and using column-stochasticity ($\sum_j(C_0)_{jk}=\sum_j\mathbf{1}_{j=k}=1$) to cancel the $g_k^2$ terms yields~\eqref{eq:dVB}.
260	
261	\emph{Step 3: $V_B'(0)\leq 0$ (universal, no conditions on $C_0$).}
262	At $\delta{=}0$: $\mu_j(0)=g_j$ (since $C(0)=I$), so $(g_k-\mu_j(0))^2=(g_k-g_j)^2$.
263	The diagonal terms ($j{=}k$) vanish.
264	Hence
265	\[
266	V_B'(0)=-\sum_{j\neq k}(C_0)_{jk}\,p_k\,(g_j-g_k)^2\;\leq\; 0,
267	\]
268	since every term is non-negative.
269	
270	\emph{Step 4: $V_B'(1)\leq 0$ for $K{=}2$.}
271	For $K{=}2$ with $C_0=\bigl[\begin{smallmatrix}a&1{-}b\\1{-}a&b\end{smallmatrix}\bigr]$ and $a,b\geq\tfrac{1}{2}$,
272	set $g_0{=}0$, $g_1{=}1$ without loss of generality
273	(the general case scales by $(\Delta g)^2$).
274	At $\delta{=}1$: $q_0{=}ap_0{+}(1{-}b)p_1$, $q_1{=}(1{-}a)p_0{+}bp_1$,
275	$\mu_0{=}(1{-}b)p_1/q_0$, $\mu_1{=}bp_1/q_1$.
276	Expanding~\eqref{eq:dVB} at $\delta{=}1$ and factoring:
277	\begin{align*}
278	  V_B'(1) &= (1{-}a)\,p_0(\mu_0^2{-}\mu_1^2)+(1{-}b)\,p_1\bigl[(1{-}\mu_1)^2{-}(1{-}\mu_0)^2\bigr] \\
279	  &= (\mu_0{-}\mu_1)\,\bigl[(1{-}a)\,p_0(\mu_0{+}\mu_1)+(1{-}b)\,p_1(2{-}\mu_0{-}\mu_1)\bigr].
280	\end{align*}
281	The bracket is non-negative (since $0\leq\mu_j\leq 1$ and all coefficients are non-negative).
282	Direct calculation gives $\mu_0{-}\mu_1=p_0\,p_1(1{-}a{-}b)/(q_0\,q_1)$
283	(expand $(1{-}b)\,q_1-b\,q_0=p_0(1{-}a{-}b)$).
284	Under $a,b\geq\tfrac{1}{2}$: $a{+}b\geq 1$, so $\mu_0{-}\mu_1\leq 0$ and $V_B'(1)\leq 0$.
285	
286	\emph{Step 5: Monotonicity from convexity.}
287	Since $V_B$ is convex (Step~1), its derivative $V_B'$ is non-decreasing.
288	When $V_B'(1)\leq 0$, we have $V_B'(\delta)\leq V_B'(1)\leq 0$ for all $\delta\in[0,1]$, so $V_B$ is non-increasing.
289	
290	\emph{Step 6: $|B(\delta)|$ is non-decreasing.}
291	$V_W(\delta)=\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B(\delta)$ is non-decreasing (Step~5).
292	The function $h(x)=|\lambda|\,x/(x+\sigma_T^2)$ is strictly increasing for $x\geq 0$.
293	Hence $|B(\delta)|=h(V_W(\delta))$ is non-decreasing. \qed
294	
295	\paragraph{Condition on $C_0$.}
296	The proof requires $V_B'(1)\leq 0$, which is guaranteed for $K{=}2$ when $(C_0)_{jj}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}$ (Step~4).
297	For $K\geq 3$, $V_B'(1)\leq 0$ holds for all $g$ and $p$ if and only if a certain matrix $M-W$ (depending on $C_0$ and $p$) is negative semi-definite; simple diagonal thresholds are sufficient in practice but not universal.
298	Numerical verification across 20{,}000 random $(C_0,p,g)$ triples per $K\in\{3,5,7\}$ with $(C_0)_{jj}\geq 1/K$ finds worst-case $|B|$ step deviations $< 0.013$ (Table~\ref{tab:conjecture_verify}), confirming $\varepsilon$-approximate monotonicity.
299	A $K{=}2$ counterexample ($C_0$ the permutation matrix, $(C_0)_{jj}{=}0$) shows the diagonal condition is necessary: $V_B(\delta)=(\delta{-}\tfrac{1}{2})^2$ increases on $(\tfrac{1}{2},1]$.
300	
301	\paragraph{General case (non-proportional effects).}
302	$|B(C(\delta))|\leq\bar{B}(\delta)$ where $\bar{B}(\delta)=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(g\mid A^*_\delta)]\,\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(f\mid A^*_\delta)]}\,/\,D(\delta)$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:conf_gen}).
303	By the same convexity argument, each $\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(\cdot\mid A^*_\delta)]$ is non-decreasing in $\delta$, and $D(\delta)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*_\delta)]$ is non-decreasing.
304	Extended verification (20{,}000 random $C_0$ matrices, $K \in \{3,5,7\}$) reveals worst-case step deviations of 0.011 (confounding), confirming approximate tightness; a strict monotonicity proof for the general case remains open.
305	
306	\begin{remark}[Conditional exact monotonicity]
307	\label{rem:conditional_monotonicity}
308	Exact monotonicity ($V_B'(\delta) \leq 0$ for all $\delta$) holds under the \emph{component-wise diagonal dominance} condition:
309	\begin{equation}
310	  \alpha_j \geq \frac{q_j}{p_j + q_j} \quad \text{for all } j,
311	  \label{eq:dd_condition}
312	\end{equation}
313	where $\alpha_j = (C_0)_{jj}$ and $q_j = \sum_k (C_0)_{jk} p_k$. This condition requires that each class's classification accuracy exceeds a threshold determined by its spillover ratio $q_j/p_j$. The proof uses Cauchy--Schwarz on the endpoint derivative decomposition $V_B'(1) = \sum_j (p_j + q_j)\,e_j^2 - \sum_{j \neq k} (C_0)_{jk}\,p_k\,(g_j - g_k)^2$, where $e_j = g_j - \mu_j$ (full proof in supplementary material).
314	
315	For doubly stochastic $C_0$ with uniform $p$, condition~\eqref{eq:dd_condition} reduces to $\alpha_j \geq 1/2$, which for $K{=}2$ recovers the exact condition in Theorem~\ref{thm:monotone}. Numerical testing ($10^5$ random column-stochastic matrices with $\alpha_j \geq 0.5$) shows the condition holds in $99.3\%$ of cases; the $0.7\%$ violations arise predominantly under class imbalance with maximum step deviation $< 0.001$. When the condition fails, a quantitative bound $V_B'(1) \leq (1{-}c)^2 R_g^2$ (where $c = \min_j \alpha_j$ and $R_g = \max g_k - \min g_k$) ensures violations vanish as classifier accuracy improves.
316	\end{remark}
317	
318	\subsection{Mediation Topology ($A$ = Mediator)}
319	\label{app:mediation}
320	
321	\paragraph{Data-generating process.}
322	$T\to A\to Y$, $T\to Y$.
323	\begin{align*}
324	  T &\sim \operatorname{Bernoulli}(0.5),\\
325	  A\mid T &\sim \operatorname{Categorical}\bigl(\operatorname{softmax}(\boldsymbol{\gamma}+\boldsymbol{\delta}\,T)\bigr),\\
326	  Y &= \tau_{\mathrm{dir}}\,T + f(A) + \varepsilon_Y.
327	\end{align*}
328	
329	\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}
330	By Lemma~\ref{lem:bias_decomp}, the direct-effect estimator satisfies:
331	\begin{equation}
332	  \operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}(C) = \tau_{\mathrm{dir}} + \frac{\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,\,f(A)\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.
333	  \label{eq:med_bias}
334	\end{equation}
335	
336	The key structural difference from confounding: $T$ causes $A$, so the residual covariance $\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)$ captures the indirect effect of $T$ on $Y$ through $A$ that is not absorbed by conditioning on $A^*$.
337	
338	\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}
339	
340	At $C=I$ ($A^*{=}A$): conditioning on $A$ blocks the mediated path $T\to A\to Y$, so $\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A)=0$ and $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau=\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$.
341	
342	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$ ($A^*\perp A$): no information about $A$ is used, and
343	\begin{align*}
344	  \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)] &= \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)),\\
345	  D(C) &= \operatorname{Var}(T) = 0.25.
346	\end{align*}
347	The indirect effect is $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}=\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0]$, and
348	\[
349	  \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)) = 0.25\,\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}.
350	\]
351	Hence at $C$ uniform: $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau = \tau_{\mathrm{dir}}+\tau_{\mathrm{ind}} = \tau_{\mathrm{total}}$.
352	
353	\paragraph{Numerical Verification (Mediation sign preservation).}
354	\label{find:med_sign}
355	$\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})$ whenever $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$, where $\tau_{\mathrm{total}}=\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}+\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$.
356	This result is verified numerically for $100{,}000$ independently sampled column-stochastic matrices $C$; a closed-form proof for general $K$ remains open.
357	
358	\begin{proof}
359	Let $R(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*)]$ and $D(C)=\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]>0$.
360	
361	\emph{Endpoints.}
362	At $C{=}I$: $f(A)$ is constant given $A{=}A^*$, so $R(I)=0$ and $B(I)=0$.
363	At $C=\frac{1}{K}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^\top$: $R=\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))=0.25\,\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ and $D=\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.25$, giving $B=\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$.
364	
365	\emph{Within-group structure for binary $T$.}
366	Since $T\in\{0,1\}$, the within-$A^*$-group bias admits a revealing decomposition.
367	Let $p_j=P(T{=}1\mid A^*{=}j)$.
368	Then $\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*{=}j)=p_j(1{-}p_j)$ and
369	\[
370	  \operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\mid A^*{=}j)
371	  = p_j(1{-}p_j)\bigl[\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]\bigr].
372	\]
373	Define $\delta_j=\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]$ as the \emph{conditional indirect effect} in the $A^*{=}j$ subpopulation.
374	Then
375	\[
376	  B(C)=\frac{R(C)}{D(C)}
377	  =\frac{\sum_j q_j\,p_j(1{-}p_j)\,\delta_j}{\sum_j q_j\,p_j(1{-}p_j)}
378	  =\sum_j w_j\,\delta_j,
379	\]
380	a weighted average of the conditional indirect effects, with weights $w_j=q_j p_j(1{-}p_j)/\sum_\ell q_\ell p_\ell(1{-}p_\ell)\geq 0$ summing to 1.
381	
382	At $C{=}I$: each $A^*{=}j$ group has $A{=}j$ a.s., so $\delta_j=f(j)-f(j)=0$ and $B=0$.
383	At $C$ uniform: $\delta_j=\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ for all $j$ and $B=\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$.
384	
385	\emph{Sign preservation.}
386	When $\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))>0$ (the indirect effect reinforces the direct effect, i.e., $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ have the same sign), the bias $B(C)\geq 0$ cannot oppose $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$, so sign is preserved trivially.
387	
388	When $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ have opposite signs but $|\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}|<|\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}|$ (i.e., $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$), sign reversal requires some $|\delta_j|>|\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}|$ with the opposing sign.
389	Since each $\delta_j$ is a difference of weighted averages of $f$ values (with weights $P(A{=}k\mid T{=}t,A^*{=}j)$), and $B(C)$ is a convex combination of $\delta_j$, the bound $|B(C)|\leq\max_j|\delta_j|$ holds.
390	We verify numerically that $\max_j|\delta_j|\leq|\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}|$ for all 100{,}000 random $C$ matrices in our experiments, confirming sign preservation.
391	\end{proof}
392	
393	\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}
394	With $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}{=}0.5$, $\boldsymbol{\gamma}{=}(0,0,0)$, $\boldsymbol{\delta}{=}(0,0.8,{-}0.6)$, $\boldsymbol{\beta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.5)$:
395	
396	$\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}=\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}1]-\mathbb{E}[f(A)\mid T{=}0]=0.351$ (computed from the softmax class probabilities).
397	$\tau_{\mathrm{total}}=0.5+0.351=0.851>0$.
398	Since $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}{>}0$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{total}}{>}0$, sign preservation holds for all $C$.
399	
400	\paragraph{Mediation monotonicity (proportional effects).}
401	\label{par:med_monotone}
402	
403	Under the interpolation $C(\delta)=(1{-}\delta)I+\delta C_0$, we prove $|B(C(\delta))|$ is non-decreasing in $\delta$ when the mediator's effect on $Y$ is proportional to its informativeness about $T$: $f(A)=\lambda\,g(A)$ for some scalar $\lambda$, where $g(k)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A{=}k]$.
404	
405	\emph{Step 1: Decompose $T$ via the mediator.}
406	Define $g(A)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A]$ and $\varepsilon_T = T - g(A)$.
407	By construction, $\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_T\mid A]=0$.
408	By non-differential misclassification ($T\perp A^*\mid A$):
409	$\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_T\mid A{=}k, A^*{=}j] = 0$,
410	so $\mathbb{E}[\varepsilon_T\mid A^*{=}j]=0$ and $\operatorname{Cov}(\varepsilon_T,\,g(A)\mid A^*{=}j)=0$ for every $j$.
411	
412	\emph{Step 2: Reduce the numerator.}
413	Since $f=\lambda g$:
414	\begin{align*}
415	  R(C) &= \lambda\,\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(g(A)\mid A^*)] = \lambda\,V_W.
416	\end{align*}
417	
418	\emph{Step 3: Constant additive term in denominator.}
419	\begin{align*}
420	  D(C) &= \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A^*)]
421	       = V_W + \sigma_\varepsilon^2,
422	\end{align*}
423	where $\sigma_\varepsilon^2\equiv\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_T\mid A^*)]$.
424	This is constant (independent of $C$):
425	$\mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(\varepsilon_T\mid A^*)]
426	  = \sum_k p_k\,\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A{=}k)
427	  = \mathbb{E}[\operatorname{Var}(T\mid A)]$,
428	where the equality uses column-stochasticity: $\sum_j q_j\,P(A{=}k\mid A^*{=}j)=p_k$.
429	
430	\emph{Step 4: Closed form and monotonicity.}
431	$B(C) = \lambda\,V_W/(V_W+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)$,
432	structurally identical to confounding (Proposition~\ref{prop:conf_prop}) with $\sigma_\varepsilon^2$ playing the role of $\sigma_T^2$.
433	Since $V_B(\delta)$ is convex with $V_B'(0)\leq 0$ (same argument as \S\ref{app:monotonicity_proof}, Steps~1--3), $V_W(\delta)=V{-}V_B(\delta)$ is non-decreasing under the same conditions on $C_0$.
434	Since $h(x)=|\lambda|x/(x+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)$ is increasing, $|B(C(\delta))|$ is non-decreasing.
435	
436	\paragraph{Boundedness of the leakage coefficient.}
437	
438	The mediation bias~\eqref{eq:mediation} writes the residual indirect effect as $(1-\lambda(C))\,\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$, which (for $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}\neq 0$) implicitly defines
439	\begin{equation}
440	\label{eq:lambda_def}
441	  \lambda(C) \;:=\; 1-\frac{B(C)}{\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}},\qquad
442	  B(C)\;\equiv\;\operatorname{plim}\hat{\tau}_{\mathrm{dir}}(A^*)-\tau_{\mathrm{dir}},\quad
443	  \tau_{\mathrm{ind}}\;=\;\frac{\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))}{\operatorname{Var}(T)}.
444	\end{equation}
445	Substituting back into~\eqref{eq:mediation} gives the convex-combination form
446	$\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}(C)=\lambda(C)\,\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}+(1-\lambda(C))\,\tau_{\mathrm{total}}$, so $\lambda(C)$ is the weight placing the biased plim between $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ (perfect classification) and $\tau_{\mathrm{total}}$ (no information).
447	
448	\begin{proposition}[Mediation leakage boundedness]
449	\label{prop:lambda_bound}
450	Let $\lambda(C)$ be defined by~\eqref{eq:lambda_def} with $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}\neq 0$, and let $C$ be column-stochastic.
451	\begin{enumerate}
452	\item[(a)] $\lambda(C)\leq 1$ iff $\operatorname{sign}(B(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{ind}})$ (the bias points in the direction of the indirect effect; no overcorrection).
453	\item[(b)] $\lambda(C)\geq 0$ iff additionally $|B(C)|\leq|\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}|$ (the bias does not exceed the indirect effect in magnitude).
454	\end{enumerate}
455	Equivalently, $\lambda(C)\in[0,1]$ iff $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}(C)\in[\min(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}},\tau_{\mathrm{total}}),\,\max(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}},\tau_{\mathrm{total}})]$.
456	
457	Under \emph{proportional effects} ($f(A)=\lambda_0\,g(A)$ with $g(k)=\mathbb{E}[T\mid A{=}k]$), both (a) and (b) hold for every $C$, so $\lambda(C)\in[0,1]$ unconditionally.
458	For \emph{general (non-proportional)} $f$, both (a) and (b) can fail because $\operatorname{Cov}(\mathbb{E}[g\mid A^*],\mathbb{E}[f\mid A^*])$ may differ in sign or magnitude from $\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)$ (a covariance-level analogue of Simpson's paradox under $A^*$ aggregation); the numerical verification below quantifies the empirical rate.
459	The endpoints $\lambda(I)=1$ and $\lambda(C)\to 0$ in the rank-one (uninformative) limit hold in all regimes.
460	
461	\emph{Sign preservation.} When $\lambda(C)\in[0,1]$, the convex-combination form gives $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}(C)\in[\min(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}},\tau_{\mathrm{total}}),\,\max(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}},\tau_{\mathrm{total}})]$. If additionally $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$, both endpoints share the same sign, so $\operatorname{sign}(\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})$ for every~$C$. Under proportional effects, $\lambda(C)\in[0,1]$ is automatic (above), so sign preservation of $\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ reduces to the single condition $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$.
462	\end{proposition}
463	
464	\begin{proof}
465	\emph{(a),(b).} With $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}\neq 0$, $\lambda(C)\leq 1\iff B(C)/\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}\geq 0\iff \operatorname{sign}(B(C))=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{ind}})$. Given (a), $\lambda(C)\geq 0\iff B(C)/\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}\leq 1\iff|B(C)|\leq|\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}|$.
466	
467	\emph{Proportional case.} Step~4 of the mediation derivation gives $B(C)=\lambda_0\,V_W(C)/(V_W(C)+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)$; the rank-one limit $V_W{\to}V$ yields $\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}=\lambda_0\,V/(V+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)$. Both quantities carry $\operatorname{sign}(\lambda_0)$, establishing~(a). The ratio
468	\[
469	  \frac{B(C)}{\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}}\;=\;\frac{V_W(C)\,(V+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)}{V\,(V_W(C)+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)}
470	\]
471	is monotone increasing in $V_W(C)\in[0,V]$ with range $[0,1]$ (its derivative in $V_W$ is $\sigma_\varepsilon^2(V+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)/[V(V_W+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)^2]>0$), establishing~(b). Hence $\lambda(C)\in[0,1]$ unconditionally.
472	
473	\emph{General case.} Substituting $\lambda(C)$ from~\eqref{eq:lambda_def} into~\eqref{eq:mediation} yields $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}(C)=\lambda(C)\,\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}+(1{-}\lambda(C))\,\tau_{\mathrm{total}}$, so $\lambda(C)\in[0,1]$ iff $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}(C)$ lies in the closed interval with endpoints $\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\tau_{\mathrm{total}}$.
474	
475	\emph{Endpoints.} At $C{=}I$, $A^*{=}A$ a.s., $\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]=g(A)$, and FWL gives $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}=\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$, hence $B(I)=0$ and $\lambda(I)=1$. At any rank-one $C$ (rows identical), $A^*\perp A$ so $\mathbb{E}[T\mid A^*]$ is constant, the partial regression on $A^*$ has no effect, and $\operatorname{plim}\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}=\operatorname{Cov}(Y,T)/\operatorname{Var}(T)=\tau_{\mathrm{total}}$, giving $B=\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ and $\lambda=0$.
476	\end{proof}
477	
478	\paragraph{Relation to the between-variance ratio.}
479	A natural \emph{diagnostic} closely related to $\lambda(C)$ is the between-variance ratio
480	\begin{equation}
481	\label{eq:vb_ratio}
482	  \lambda_V(C) \;:=\; \frac{V_B(C)}{V},\qquad V_B(C)\;=\;\operatorname{Var}\!\bigl(\mathbb{E}[g(A)\mid A^*]\bigr),\quad V\;=\;\operatorname{Var}(g(A)),
483	\end{equation}
484	which satisfies $\lambda_V(C)\in[0,1]$ \emph{unconditionally} by the law of total variance applied to $g(A)$. Under proportional effects, $\lambda$ and $\lambda_V$ agree at the endpoints and are related by
485	\[
486	  1-\lambda(C)\;=\;\frac{V_W(C)\,(V+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)}{V\,(V_W(C)+\sigma_\varepsilon^2)}\;=\;\bigl(1-\lambda_V(C)\bigr)\cdot\frac{V+\sigma_\varepsilon^2}{V_W(C)+\sigma_\varepsilon^2},
487	\]
488	so they coincide in the limit $\sigma_\varepsilon^2\gg V$. They generally differ for non-proportional $f$. We use $\lambda$ throughout the main text because it parameterizes~\eqref{eq:mediation} directly; $\lambda_V$ remains a useful summary of the channel quality of $A^*$ as a proxy for $A$ (independent of the structural form of $f$).
489	
490	\paragraph{Numerical verification.}
491	For each $K\in\{3,5,7\}$ we draw $N{=}10{,}000$ configurations $(\pi,g,\sigma_\varepsilon^2,C)$ with $\pi\sim\operatorname{Dirichlet}(\mathbf{1})$, $g\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_K)$, $\sigma_\varepsilon^2\sim\operatorname{Uniform}(0.1,2)$, columns of $C\sim\operatorname{Dirichlet}(\mathbf{1})$, and evaluate $\lambda(C)$ from~\eqref{eq:lambda_def} in two regimes for $f$:
492	\begin{itemize}\itemsep0pt
493	\item \textbf{Proportional} ($f=\lambda_0\,g$, $\lambda_0\sim\mathcal{N}(0,1)\!\setminus\!\{0\}$): $\lambda(C)\in[0,1]$ in \emph{all} $30{,}000$ trials (pooled observed range $[1.2{\times}10^{-5},\,0.850]$; zero out-of-bounds), confirming the closed-form bound. Sign preservation of $\hat\tau_{\mathrm{dir}}$ therefore reduces to $\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{dir}})=\operatorname{sign}(\tau_{\mathrm{total}})$.
494	\item \textbf{Non-proportional} ($f\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_K)$ drawn independently of $g$, restricted to $|\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}|\geq 0.05$ to avoid the ill-conditioned regime where $\lambda{=}1{-}B/\tau_{\mathrm{ind}}$ diverges): $\lambda(C)$ exceeds $[0,1]$ in $\sim15$--$17\%$ of trials, illustrating that (a) and (b) are not automatic outside the proportional case.
495	\end{itemize}
496	The diagnostic $\lambda_V(C)$ lies in $[0,1]$ across all $30{,}000$ trials irrespective of regime, as guaranteed by the law of total variance. The script and full results are in \texttt{artifacts/verify\_lambda\_bound.py} and \texttt{artifacts/verify\_lambda\_bound\_results.json}.
497	
498	\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:k3_symmetric} ($K{=}3$ Symmetric Monotonicity)}
499	\label{app:k3_symmetric_proof}
500	
```

      "content": "\\section{Proofs of Proposition~\\ref{prop:signflip} and Theorem~\\ref{thm:monotone}}\n\\label{app:proof}\n\nWe provide algebraic proofs of sign preservation for the confounding and front-door (Numerical Verification~1), and the monotonicity proof for confounding (Theorem~\\ref{thm:monotone}).\nThroughout, $A\\in\\{0,1,\\dots,K{-}1\\}$ with prior $p_k=P(A{=}k)$, and $C$ is a $K{\\times}K$ column-stochastic confusion matrix with $C_{jk}=P(A^*{=}j\\mid A{=}k)$.\nWe use category~0 as the reference and let $D_j=\\mathbf{1}(A{=}j)$, $D_j^*=\\mathbf{1}(A^*{=}j)$ for population vectors:\n\\begin{align}\n  q_j &= P(A^*{=}j) = \\textstyle\\sum_k C_{jk}\\,p_k, \\label{eq:q}\\\\\n  \\mathrm{ct}_j &= \\mathbb{E}[T\\cdot D_j^*] = \\textstyle\\sum_k C_{jk}\\,\\mathbb{E}[T\\cdot D_k], \\label{eq:ct}\\\\\n  \\mathrm{cy}_j &= \\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D_j^*] = \\textstyle\\sum_k C_{jk}\\,\\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D_k]. \\label{eq:cy}\n\\end{align}\nWrite $q = Cp$, $\\mathrm{ct} = C\\,\\mathbb{E}[T\\cdot D]$, and $\\mathrm{cy} = C\\,\\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D]$, where $D=(D_0,\\dots,D_{K-1})^\\top$.\n\n\\subsection{Frisch--Waugh--Lovell Decomposition}\n\\label{app:fwl}\n\nFor topologies where $T$ is directly observed (confounding, mediation, collider, M-bias, front-door), the estimator regresses $Y$ on $Z^*=(1,T,D_1^*,\\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.\nBy the Frisch--Waugh--Lovell (FWL) theorem, the coefficient on $T$ satisfies:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat{\\tau}(C)\n  = \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}\\,\\tilde{Y}]}{\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]},\n  \\label{eq:fwl}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\tilde{T}=T-\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*]$ and $\\tilde{Y}=Y-\\mathbb{E}[Y\\mid A^*]$ are residuals from projecting onto $(1,D_1^*,\\dots,D_{K-1}^*)$.\n\n\\begin{lemma}[Bias decomposition]\n\\label{lem:bias_decomp}\nSuppose $Y = \\tau T + f(A) + \\varepsilon_Y$ with $\\varepsilon_Y \\perp (T, A, A^*)$.\nThen\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C) = \\tau + \\frac{R(C)}{D(C)},\n  \\label{eq:bias_decomp}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere\n\\begin{align}\n  R(C) &= \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,f(A)\\mid A^*)\\bigr], \\label{eq:R}\\\\\n  D(C) &= \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)\\bigr] > 0. \\label{eq:D}\n\\end{align}\n\\end{lemma}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nExpand $\\tilde{Y}$ using $Y=\\tau T + f(A) + \\varepsilon_Y$:\n\\[\n  \\tilde{Y} = \\tau\\,\\tilde{T} + \\bigl(f(A) - \\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr) + \\varepsilon_Y.\n\\]\nSince $\\tilde{T}$ is measurable w.r.t.\\ $(T,A^*)$ and $\\varepsilon_Y\\perp(T,A,A^*)$:\n\\[\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}\\,\\tilde{Y}]\n  = \\tau\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]\n  + \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\tilde{T}\\,\\bigl(f(A)-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr)\\bigr].\n\\]\nThe numerator residual is $R(C) = \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}(f(A)-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*])]$, and the denominator is $D(C) = \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]$.\n\nBy the law of total covariance:\n\\[\n  R(C) = \\sum_{j=0}^{K-1} q_j\\,\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,f(A)\\mid A^*{=}j)\n  = \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)].\n\\]\nFor $D(C)>0$: decompose $T = g(A) + \\varepsilon_T$ where $g(A)=\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A]$ (for confounding) or simply note that $T$ has exogenous variation independent of $A^*$ (for mediation).\nIn both cases, $\\tilde{T}=\\bigl(g(A)-\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr)+\\varepsilon_T'$, where $\\varepsilon_T'$ is the component of $T$ independent of $(A,A^*)$.\nHence $D(C)\\geq\\operatorname{Var}(\\varepsilon_T')>0$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\subsection{Confounding Topology ($A$ = Confounder)}\n\\label{app:confounding}\n\n\\paragraph{Data-generating process.}\n$A\\to T$, $A\\to Y$, $T\\to Y$.\nUsing dummy coding with category 0 as reference:\n\\begin{align*}\n  T &= \\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\\alpha_j D_j + \\varepsilon_T,\\quad\n  \\varepsilon_T\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,\\sigma_T^2),\\;\\varepsilon_T\\perp A,\\\\\n  Y &= \\tau\\, T + \\sum_{j=1}^{K-1}\\beta_j^{(A)} D_j + \\varepsilon_Y,\\quad\n  \\varepsilon_Y\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,1),\\;\\varepsilon_Y\\perp(T,A).\n\\end{align*}\nDefine $g(k)=\\sum_{j}\\alpha_j\\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$ and $f(k)=\\sum_{j}\\beta_j^{(A)}\\mathbf{1}(k{=}j)$, so $g(0)=f(0)=0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Closed-form plim.}\nUsing the FWL decomposition, the coefficient on $T$ is:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C)\n  = \\frac{\\mu_{TY} - \\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\\frac{\\mathrm{ct}_j\\,\\mathrm{cy}_j}{q_j}}\n  {s_T - \\sum_{j=0}^{K-1}\\frac{\\mathrm{ct}_j^2}{q_j}},\n  \\label{eq:plim_conf}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $s_T=\\mathbb{E}[T^2]$, $\\mu_{TY}=\\mathbb{E}[TY]$, and $\\mathrm{ct}_j$, $\\mathrm{cy}_j$, $q_j$ are defined in~\\eqref{eq:q}--\\eqref{eq:cy}.\n\n\\begin{proof}[Derivation]\nBy FWL, $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau = \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}Y]/\\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]$ where $\\tilde{T}=T-\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*]$.\nSince $\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*{=}j]=\\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j$:\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}^2]\n  &= \\mathbb{E}[T^2] - \\sum_j q_j\\bigl(\\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\\bigr)^2\n  = s_T - \\sum_j \\mathrm{ct}_j^2/q_j,\\\\\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\tilde{T}\\,Y]\n  &= \\mathbb{E}[TY] - \\sum_j \\bigl(\\mathrm{ct}_j/q_j\\bigr)\\,\\mathbb{E}[Y\\cdot D_j^*]\n  = \\mu_{TY} - \\sum_j \\mathrm{ct}_j\\,\\mathrm{cy}_j/q_j.\n\\end{align*}\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Verification at $C=I$.}\nWhen $C=I$: $q_j=p_j$, $\\mathrm{ct}_j=\\mathbb{E}[T D_j]=\\alpha_j p_j$, $\\mathrm{cy}_j=\\mathbb{E}[Y D_j]=p_j(\\tau\\alpha_j+\\beta_j^{(A)})$.\nDirect substitution yields numerator $= \\tau\\sigma_T^2$ and denominator $= \\sigma_T^2$, giving $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau = \\tau$.\n\n\\paragraph{Bias structure.}\nBy Lemma~\\ref{lem:bias_decomp}:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C) = \\tau + B(C),\\quad\n  B(C) = \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\\,f(A)\\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.\n  \\label{eq:bias_conf}\n\\end{equation}\nBy the law of total covariance:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\\mid A^*)]\n  = \\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))\n  - \\operatorname{Cov}\\bigl(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*],\\;\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr).\n  \\label{eq:lotc}\n\\end{equation}\n\nAt $C=I$: $\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*]=g(A)$, so the between-group covariance equals $\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)$ and $B(I)=0$.\n\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$ (uniform, $A^*\\perp A$): $\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*]$ is constant, so the between-group covariance is zero and\n\\begin{equation}\n  B_{\\mathrm{OV}} \\;\\equiv\\; B\\bigl(\\tfrac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top\\bigr)\n  = \\frac{\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),\\,f(A))}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)},\n  \\label{eq:bov}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich equals the classical omitted-variable bias (no control for $A$).\n\n\\paragraph{Sign preservation condition.}\nSince $D(C)>0$, sign$(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau) = $ sign$(\\tau\\cdot D(C) + R(C))$.\nFor sign preservation, it suffices that $R(C)$ does not overwhelm $\\tau\\cdot D(C)$.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- proportional case]\n\\label{prop:conf_prop}\nIf $f(A) = \\lambda\\,g(A)$ for some constant $\\lambda$ (i.e., the confounder effects on $T$ and $Y$ are proportional), then\n\\begin{equation}\n  B(C) = \\bigl(1-\\xi(C)\\bigr)\\,B_{\\mathrm{OV}},\n  \\label{eq:convex}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere\n\\[\n  \\xi(C) = \\frac{\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*])\\;\\sigma_T^2}\n  {\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\\bigl(\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))-\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*])+\\sigma_T^2\\bigr)}\n  \\;\\in\\;[0,1].\n\\]\nConsequently, $B(C)$ lies between $0$ and $B_{\\mathrm{OV}}$, and $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ whenever $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau+B_{\\mathrm{OV}})$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nWhen $f=\\lambda g$: the between-group covariance in~\\eqref{eq:lotc} becomes $\\lambda\\,\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])$, and $\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)=\\lambda\\,\\operatorname{Var}(g)$.\nSo $R(C)=\\lambda\\bigl(\\operatorname{Var}(g)-\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])\\bigr)=\\lambda\\,V_W$, where $V_W=\\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B$ with $V_B=\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*])$.\nSimilarly, $D(C)=V_W+\\sigma_T^2$.\nHence\n\\[\n  B(C) = \\frac{\\lambda\\,V_W}{V_W+\\sigma_T^2},\\qquad\n  B_{\\mathrm{OV}} = \\frac{\\lambda\\,V}{V+\\sigma_T^2},\n\\]\nwhere $V=\\operatorname{Var}(g)=V_B+V_W$.\nThe ratio\n\\[\n  \\frac{B(C)}{B_{\\mathrm{OV}}} = \\frac{V_W(V+\\sigma_T^2)}{V(V_W+\\sigma_T^2)} = 1 - \\xi(C)\n\\]\nis a decreasing function of $V_B\\in[0,V]$.\nAt $V_B=0$ (no classification, $C$ uniform): $\\xi=0$, $B=B_{\\mathrm{OV}}$.\nAt $V_B=V$ (perfect classification, $C=I$): $\\xi=1$, $B=0$.\nSince $0\\leq V_B\\leq V$, we have $\\xi\\in[0,1]$ and $B(C)\\in[0,B_{\\mathrm{OV}}]$ (with the sign of $B_{\\mathrm{OV}}$).\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Confounding sign preservation --- general case]\n\\label{prop:conf_gen}\nFor general $g$ and $f$ (not necessarily proportional), define the worst-case bias bound\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\bar{B} \\;=\\; \\frac{\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f(A))}}{\\sigma_T^2}.\n  \\label{eq:bbar}\n\\end{equation}\nIf $|\\tau| > \\bar{B}$, then $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau(C))=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ for \\emph{all} column-stochastic $C$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}\nWe need $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau\\cdot D(C)+R(C))=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$, which holds if $|R(C)|<|\\tau|\\cdot D(C)$.\nSince $D(C)\\geq\\sigma_T^2$ (shown in Lemma~\\ref{lem:bias_decomp}), it suffices to show $|R(C)|<|\\tau|\\,\\sigma_T^2$.\n\n\\emph{Step 1: Bound $|R(C)|$.}\n$R(C)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A)\\mid A^*)]$.\nBy the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality applied to each within-group covariance:\n\\[\n  |\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f\\mid A^*{=}j)|\n  \\leq \\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g\\mid A^*{=}j)\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f\\mid A^*{=}j)}.\n\\]\nTaking expectations and applying Cauchy--Schwarz again:\n\\begin{align*}\n  |R(C)|\n  &\\leq \\mathbb{E}\\bigl[\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g\\mid A^*)\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f\\mid A^*)}\\bigr]\\\\\n  &\\leq \\sqrt{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(g\\mid A^*)]\\;\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(f\\mid A^*)]}\\\\\n  &\\leq \\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g(A))\\;\\operatorname{Var}(f(A))},\n\\end{align*}\nwhere the last step uses $\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(X\\mid Y)]\\leq\\operatorname{Var}(X)$ (law of total variance).\n\n\\emph{Step 2: Conclude.}\nThe bias satisfies\n\\[\n  |B(C)| = \\frac{|R(C)|}{D(C)} \\leq \\frac{\\sqrt{\\operatorname{Var}(g)\\,\\operatorname{Var}(f)}}{\\sigma_T^2} = \\bar{B}.\n\\]\nWhen $|\\tau|>\\bar{B}$, the bias cannot cancel $\\tau$, so $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)$ for all $C$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Relationship to the omitted-variable condition.}\nA weaker (necessary) condition for sign preservation is $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau)=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}})$ where $\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}}=\\tau+\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\\operatorname{Var}(T)$.\nIf this fails, then $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$ (uniform noise) already flips the sign.\nThe rigorous sufficient condition $|\\tau|>\\bar{B}$ implies the weaker condition, since $|B_{\\mathrm{OV}}|=|\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)|/\\operatorname{Var}(T)\\leq\\bar{B}$ by Cauchy--Schwarz and $\\operatorname{Var}(T)\\geq\\sigma_T^2$.\n\n\\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}\nWith $\\tau{=}2.0$, $\\boldsymbol{\\alpha}{=}(1.5,{-}1.0)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}^{(A)}{=}(1.0,{-}0.5)$, $\\sigma_T{=}1.0$, $\\mathbf{p}{=}(0.40,0.35,0.25)$:\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\operatorname{Var}(g) &= \\sum_k p_k\\alpha_k^2 - \\bigl(\\textstyle\\sum_k p_k\\alpha_k\\bigr)^2 = 0.962,\\\\\n  \\operatorname{Var}(f) &= \\sum_k p_k(\\beta_k^{(A)})^2 - \\bigl(\\textstyle\\sum_k p_k\\beta_k^{(A)}\\bigr)^2 = 0.362,\\\\\n  \\bar{B} &= \\sqrt{0.962\\times 0.362}\\;/\\;1.0^2 = 0.590.\n\\end{align*}\nSince $|\\tau|{=}2.0 > \\bar{B}{=}0.590$, sign preservation is guaranteed for \\emph{all} column-stochastic $C$ by Proposition~\\ref{prop:conf_gen}.\nAdditionally, $B_{\\mathrm{OV}}=\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)/\\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.588/1.962={+}0.300$, giving $\\tau_{\\mathrm{OV}}{=}2.300>0$, confirming that both the necessary and sufficient conditions are satisfied.\n\n\\paragraph{Extension to $K > 3$.}\nFor $K{>}3$, treatment effect coefficients are drawn from a fixed pool with alternating signs and decreasing magnitudes: $\\boldsymbol{\\beta} = (0, 1.0, {-}0.5, 0.7, {-}0.3, 0.5, {-}0.2, 0.4, {-}0.15, 0.3, \\ldots)$, truncated to $K$ entries. The category distribution uses $p_k \\propto 1/(k{+}1)$, normalized to sum to 1. Other topology-specific parameters (confounding strengths, mediator effects, etc.) follow analogous alternating-sign pools. For $K{=}10$, $\\boldsymbol{\\beta} = (0, 1.0, {-}0.5, 0.7, {-}0.3, 0.5, {-}0.2, 0.4, {-}0.15, 0.3)$.\n\n\\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\\ref{thm:monotone} (Confounding Monotonicity)}\n\\label{app:monotonicity_proof}\n\nUnder the interpolation $C(\\delta)=(1{-}\\delta)I+\\delta C_0$, we prove $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is non-decreasing in $\\delta$ for proportional effects ($f=\\lambda g$) and provide numerical verification for the general case.\nThe proof proceeds by direct algebraic analysis of $V_B(\\delta)=\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*_\\delta])$ as a rational function of $\\delta$, establishing convexity and bounding the derivative at both endpoints.\n\n\\paragraph{Proportional case ($f=\\lambda g$).}\nFrom Proposition~\\ref{prop:conf_prop}, $B(C)=\\lambda V_W/(V_W+\\sigma_T^2)$ where $V_W=\\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B$ and $V_B(\\delta)=\\operatorname{Var}(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*_\\delta])$.\n\n\\emph{Step 1: Convexity of $V_B(\\delta)$.}\nDefine $N_j(\\delta)=\\sum_k g_k\\,C(\\delta)_{jk}\\,p_k$ and $q_j(\\delta)=\\sum_k C(\\delta)_{jk}\\,p_k$.\nBoth are affine in $\\delta$, with $q_j(\\delta)>0$.\nThen $V_B(\\delta)=\\sum_j N_j(\\delta)^2/q_j(\\delta) - \\bar{g}^2$.\nEach term $N_j^2/q_j$ is the composition of the perspective function $(x,t)\\mapsto x^2/t$ (convex for $t>0$) with the affine map $\\delta\\mapsto(N_j(\\delta),\\,q_j(\\delta))$, hence convex in $\\delta$.\nA sum of convex functions is convex, so $V_B(\\delta)$ is convex on $[0,\\delta_{\\max}]$.\n\n\\emph{Step 2: Derivative formula.}\nLet $\\mu_j(\\delta)=N_j(\\delta)/q_j(\\delta)=\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*{=}j]$.\nThen\n\\begin{equation}\n\\frac{dV_B}{d\\delta}\n= -\\sum_{j,k}\\bigl[(C_0)_{jk}-\\mathbf{1}_{j=k}\\bigr]\\,p_k\\,(g_k-\\mu_j(\\delta))^2.\n\\label{eq:dVB}\n\\end{equation}\n\\emph{Derivation.}\n$\\frac{d}{d\\delta}\\frac{N_j^2}{q_j}=2\\mu_j\\dot{N}_j-\\mu_j^2\\dot{q}_j$\nwhere $\\dot{N}_j=m_j^0-g_jp_j$ and $\\dot{q}_j=q_j^0-p_j$ (with $m_j^0=\\sum_k g_k(C_0)_{jk}p_k$, $q_j^0=\\sum_k(C_0)_{jk}p_k$).\nSubstituting and using column-stochasticity ($\\sum_j(C_0)_{jk}=\\sum_j\\mathbf{1}_{j=k}=1$) to cancel the $g_k^2$ terms yields~\\eqref{eq:dVB}.\n\n\\emph{Step 3: $V_B'(0)\\leq 0$ (universal, no conditions on $C_0$).}\nAt $\\delta{=}0$: $\\mu_j(0)=g_j$ (since $C(0)=I$), so $(g_k-\\mu_j(0))^2=(g_k-g_j)^2$.\nThe diagonal terms ($j{=}k$) vanish.\nHence\n\\[\nV_B'(0)=-\\sum_{j\\neq k}(C_0)_{jk}\\,p_k\\,(g_j-g_k)^2\\;\\leq\\; 0,\n\\]\nsince every term is non-negative.\n\n\\emph{Step 4: $V_B'(1)\\leq 0$ for $K{=}2$.}\nFor $K{=}2$ with $C_0=\\bigl[\\begin{smallmatrix}a&1{-}b\\\\1{-}a&b\\end{smallmatrix}\\bigr]$ and $a,b\\geq\\tfrac{1}{2}$,\nset $g_0{=}0$, $g_1{=}1$ without loss of generality\n(the general case scales by $(\\Delta g)^2$).\nAt $\\delta{=}1$: $q_0{=}ap_0{+}(1{-}b)p_1$, $q_1{=}(1{-}a)p_0{+}bp_1$,\n$\\mu_0{=}(1{-}b)p_1/q_0$, $\\mu_1{=}bp_1/q_1$.\nExpanding~\\eqref{eq:dVB} at $\\delta{=}1$ and factoring:\n\\begin{align*}\n  V_B'(1) &= (1{-}a)\\,p_0(\\mu_0^2{-}\\mu_1^2)+(1{-}b)\\,p_1\\bigl[(1{-}\\mu_1)^2{-}(1{-}\\mu_0)^2\\bigr] \\\\\n  &= (\\mu_0{-}\\mu_1)\\,\\bigl[(1{-}a)\\,p_0(\\mu_0{+}\\mu_1)+(1{-}b)\\,p_1(2{-}\\mu_0{-}\\mu_1)\\bigr].\n\\end{align*}\nThe bracket is non-negative (since $0\\leq\\mu_j\\leq 1$ and all coefficients are non-negative).\nDirect calculation gives $\\mu_0{-}\\mu_1=p_0\\,p_1(1{-}a{-}b)/(q_0\\,q_1)$\n(expand $(1{-}b)\\,q_1-b\\,q_0=p_0(1{-}a{-}b)$).\nUnder $a,b\\geq\\tfrac{1}{2}$: $a{+}b\\geq 1$, so $\\mu_0{-}\\mu_1\\leq 0$ and $V_B'(1)\\leq 0$.\n\n\\emph{Step 5: Monotonicity from convexity.}\nSince $V_B$ is convex (Step~1), its derivative $V_B'$ is non-decreasing.\nWhen $V_B'(1)\\leq 0$, we have $V_B'(\\delta)\\leq V_B'(1)\\leq 0$ for all $\\delta\\in[0,1]$, so $V_B$ is non-increasing.\n\n\\emph{Step 6: $|B(\\delta)|$ is non-decreasing.}\n$V_W(\\delta)=\\operatorname{Var}(g)-V_B(\\delta)$ is non-decreasing (Step~5).\nThe function $h(x)=|\\lambda|\\,x/(x+\\sigma_T^2)$ is strictly increasing for $x\\geq 0$.\nHence $|B(\\delta)|=h(V_W(\\delta))$ is non-decreasing. \\qed\n\n\\paragraph{Condition on $C_0$.}\nThe proof requires $V_B'(1)\\leq 0$, which is guaranteed for $K{=}2$ when $(C_0)_{jj}\\geq\\tfrac{1}{2}$ (Step~4).\nFor $K\\geq 3$, $V_B'(1)\\leq 0$ holds for all $g$ and $p$ if and only if a certain matrix $M-W$ (depending on $C_0$ and $p$) is negative semi-definite; simple diagonal thresholds are sufficient in practice but not universal.\nNumerical verification across 20{,}000 random $(C_0,p,g)$ triples per $K\\in\\{3,5,7\\}$ with $(C_0)_{jj}\\geq 1/K$ finds worst-case $|B|$ step deviations $< 0.013$ (Table~\\ref{tab:conjecture_verify}), confirming $\\varepsilon$-approximate monotonicity.\nA $K{=}2$ counterexample ($C_0$ the permutation matrix, $(C_0)_{jj}{=}0$) shows the diagonal condition is necessary: $V_B(\\delta)=(\\delta{-}\\tfrac{1}{2})^2$ increases on $(\\tfrac{1}{2},1]$.\n\n\\paragraph{General case (non-proportional effects).}\n$|B(C(\\delta))|\\leq\\bar{B}(\\delta)$ where $\\bar{B}(\\delta)=\\sqrt{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(g\\mid A^*_\\delta)]\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(f\\mid A^*_\\delta)]}\\,/\\,D(\\delta)$ (Proposition~\\ref{prop:conf_gen}).\nBy the same convexity argument, each $\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(\\cdot\\mid A^*_\\delta)]$ is non-decreasing in $\\delta$, and $D(\\delta)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*_\\delta)]$ is non-decreasing.\nExtended verification (20{,}000 random $C_0$ matrices, $K \\in \\{3,5,7\\}$) reveals worst-case step deviations of 0.011 (confounding), confirming approximate tightness; a strict monotonicity proof for the general case remains open.\n\n\\begin{remark}[Conditional exact monotonicity]\n\\label{rem:conditional_monotonicity}\nExact monotonicity ($V_B'(\\delta) \\leq 0$ for all $\\delta$) holds under the \\emph{component-wise diagonal dominance} condition:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\alpha_j \\geq \\frac{q_j}{p_j + q_j} \\quad \\text{for all } j,\n  \\label{eq:dd_condition}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\alpha_j = (C_0)_{jj}$ and $q_j = \\sum_k (C_0)_{jk} p_k$. This condition requires that each class's classification accuracy exceeds a threshold determined by its spillover ratio $q_j/p_j$. The proof uses Cauchy--Schwarz on the endpoint derivative decomposition $V_B'(1) = \\sum_j (p_j + q_j)\\,e_j^2 - \\sum_{j \\neq k} (C_0)_{jk}\\,p_k\\,(g_j - g_k)^2$, where $e_j = g_j - \\mu_j$ (full proof in supplementary material).\n\nFor doubly stochastic $C_0$ with uniform $p$, condition~\\eqref{eq:dd_condition} reduces to $\\alpha_j \\geq 1/2$, which for $K{=}2$ recovers the exact condition in Theorem~\\ref{thm:monotone}. Numerical testing ($10^5$ random column-stochastic matrices with $\\alpha_j \\geq 0.5$) shows the condition holds in $99.3\\%$ of cases; the $0.7\\%$ violations arise predominantly under class imbalance with maximum step deviation $< 0.001$. When the condition fails, a quantitative bound $V_B'(1) \\leq (1{-}c)^2 R_g^2$ (where $c = \\min_j \\alpha_j$ and $R_g = \\max g_k - \\min g_k$) ensures violations vanish as classifier accuracy improves.\n\\end{remark}\n\n\\subsection{Mediation Topology ($A$ = Mediator)}\n\\label{app:mediation}\n\n\\paragraph{Data-generating process.}\n$T\\to A\\to Y$, $T\\to Y$.\n\\begin{align*}\n  T &\\sim \\operatorname{Bernoulli}(0.5),\\\\\n  A\\mid T &\\sim \\operatorname{Categorical}\\bigl(\\operatorname{softmax}(\\boldsymbol{\\gamma}+\\boldsymbol{\\delta}\\,T)\\bigr),\\\\\n  Y &= \\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}\\,T + f(A) + \\varepsilon_Y.\n\\end{align*}\n\n\\paragraph{Bias decomposition.}\nBy Lemma~\\ref{lem:bias_decomp}, the direct-effect estimator satisfies:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}(C) = \\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}} + \\frac{\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,\\,f(A)\\mid A^*)]}{D(C)}.\n  \\label{eq:med_bias}\n\\end{equation}\n\nThe key structural difference from confounding: $T$ causes $A$, so the residual covariance $\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)$ captures the indirect effect of $T$ on $Y$ through $A$ that is not absorbed by conditioning on $A^*$.\n\n\\paragraph{Endpoint analysis.}\n\nAt $C=I$ ($A^*{=}A$): conditioning on $A$ blocks the mediated path $T\\to A\\to Y$, so $\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A)=0$ and $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau=\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$.\n\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$ ($A^*\\perp A$): no information about $A$ is used, and\n\\begin{align*}\n  \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)] &= \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)),\\\\\n  D(C) &= \\operatorname{Var}(T) = 0.25.\n\\end{align*}\nThe indirect effect is $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}=\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0]$, and\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)) = 0.25\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}.\n\\]\nHence at $C$ uniform: $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau = \\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}+\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}} = \\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}$.\n\n\\paragraph{Numerical Verification (Mediation sign preservation).}\n\\label{find:med_sign}\n$\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})$ whenever $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$, where $\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}=\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}+\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$.\nThis result is verified numerically for $100{,}000$ independently sampled column-stochastic matrices $C$; a closed-form proof for general $K$ remains open.\n\n\\begin{proof}\nLet $R(C)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*)]$ and $D(C)=\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]>0$.\n\n\\emph{Endpoints.}\nAt $C{=}I$: $f(A)$ is constant given $A{=}A^*$, so $R(I)=0$ and $B(I)=0$.\nAt $C=\\frac{1}{K}\\mathbf{1}\\mathbf{1}^\\top$: $R=\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))=0.25\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ and $D=\\operatorname{Var}(T)=0.25$, giving $B=\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$.\n\n\\emph{Within-group structure for binary $T$.}\nSince $T\\in\\{0,1\\}$, the within-$A^*$-group bias admits a revealing decomposition.\nLet $p_j=P(T{=}1\\mid A^*{=}j)$.\nThen $\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*{=}j)=p_j(1{-}p_j)$ and\n\\[\n  \\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A)\\mid A^*{=}j)\n  = p_j(1{-}p_j)\\bigl[\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]\\bigr].\n\\]\nDefine $\\delta_j=\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1,A^*{=}j]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0,A^*{=}j]$ as the \\emph{conditional indirect effect} in the $A^*{=}j$ subpopulation.\nThen\n\\[\n  B(C)=\\frac{R(C)}{D(C)}\n  =\\frac{\\sum_j q_j\\,p_j(1{-}p_j)\\,\\delta_j}{\\sum_j q_j\\,p_j(1{-}p_j)}\n  =\\sum_j w_j\\,\\delta_j,\n\\]\na weighted average of the conditional indirect effects, with weights $w_j=q_j p_j(1{-}p_j)/\\sum_\\ell q_\\ell p_\\ell(1{-}p_\\ell)\\geq 0$ summing to 1.\n\nAt $C{=}I$: each $A^*{=}j$ group has $A{=}j$ a.s., so $\\delta_j=f(j)-f(j)=0$ and $B=0$.\nAt $C$ uniform: $\\delta_j=\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ for all $j$ and $B=\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$.\n\n\\emph{Sign preservation.}\nWhen $\\operatorname{Cov}(g(A),f(A))>0$ (the indirect effect reinforces the direct effect, i.e., $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ have the same sign), the bias $B(C)\\geq 0$ cannot oppose $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$, so sign is preserved trivially.\n\nWhen $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ have opposite signs but $|\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}|<|\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}|$ (i.e., $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$), sign reversal requires some $|\\delta_j|>|\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}|$ with the opposing sign.\nSince each $\\delta_j$ is a difference of weighted averages of $f$ values (with weights $P(A{=}k\\mid T{=}t,A^*{=}j)$), and $B(C)$ is a convex combination of $\\delta_j$, the bound $|B(C)|\\leq\\max_j|\\delta_j|$ holds.\nWe verify numerically that $\\max_j|\\delta_j|\\leq|\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}|$ for all 100{,}000 random $C$ matrices in our experiments, confirming sign preservation.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Verification for paper parameters ($K{=}3$).}\nWith $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}{=}0.5$, $\\boldsymbol{\\gamma}{=}(0,0,0)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\delta}{=}(0,0.8,{-}0.6)$, $\\boldsymbol{\\beta}{=}(0,1.0,{-}0.5)$:\n\n$\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}=\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}1]-\\mathbb{E}[f(A)\\mid T{=}0]=0.351$ (computed from the softmax class probabilities).\n$\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}=0.5+0.351=0.851>0$.\nSince $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}{>}0$ and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}{>}0$, sign preservation holds for all $C$.\n\n\\paragraph{Mediation monotonicity (proportional effects).}\n\\label{par:med_monotone}\n\nUnder the interpolation $C(\\delta)=(1{-}\\delta)I+\\delta C_0$, we prove $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is non-decreasing in $\\delta$ when the mediator's effect on $Y$ is proportional to its informativeness about $T$: $f(A)=\\lambda\\,g(A)$ for some scalar $\\lambda$, where $g(k)=\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A{=}k]$.\n\n\\emph{Step 1: Decompose $T$ via the mediator.}\nDefine $g(A)=\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A]$ and $\\varepsilon_T = T - g(A)$.\nBy construction, $\\mathbb{E}[\\varepsilon_T\\mid A]=0$.\nBy non-differential misclassification ($T\\perp A^*\\mid A$):\n$\\mathbb{E}[\\varepsilon_T\\mid A{=}k, A^*{=}j] = 0$,\nso $\\mathbb{E}[\\varepsilon_T\\mid A^*{=}j]=0$ and $\\operatorname{Cov}(\\varepsilon_T,\\,g(A)\\mid A^*{=}j)=0$ for every $j$.\n\n\\emph{Step 2: Reduce the numerator.}\nSince $f=\\lambda g$:\n\\begin{align*}\n  R(C) &= \\lambda\\,\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(g(A)\\mid A^*)] = \\lambda\\,V_W.\n\\end{align*}\n\n\\emph{Step 3: Constant additive term in denominator.}\n\\begin{align*}\n  D(C) &= \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A^*)]\n       = V_W + \\sigma_\\varepsilon^2,\n\\end{align*}\nwhere $\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2\\equiv\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(\\varepsilon_T\\mid A^*)]$.\nThis is constant (independent of $C$):\n$\\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(\\varepsilon_T\\mid A^*)]\n  = \\sum_k p_k\\,\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A{=}k)\n  = \\mathbb{E}[\\operatorname{Var}(T\\mid A)]$,\nwhere the equality uses column-stochasticity: $\\sum_j q_j\\,P(A{=}k\\mid A^*{=}j)=p_k$.\n\n\\emph{Step 4: Closed form and monotonicity.}\n$B(C) = \\lambda\\,V_W/(V_W+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)$,\nstructurally identical to confounding (Proposition~\\ref{prop:conf_prop}) with $\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2$ playing the role of $\\sigma_T^2$.\nSince $V_B(\\delta)$ is convex with $V_B'(0)\\leq 0$ (same argument as \\S\\ref{app:monotonicity_proof}, Steps~1--3), $V_W(\\delta)=V{-}V_B(\\delta)$ is non-decreasing under the same conditions on $C_0$.\nSince $h(x)=|\\lambda|x/(x+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)$ is increasing, $|B(C(\\delta))|$ is non-decreasing.\n\n\\paragraph{Boundedness of the leakage coefficient.}\n\nThe mediation bias~\\eqref{eq:mediation} writes the residual indirect effect as $(1-\\lambda(C))\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$, which (for $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}\\neq 0$) implicitly defines\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:lambda_def}\n  \\lambda(C) \\;:=\\; 1-\\frac{B(C)}{\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}},\\qquad\n  B(C)\\;\\equiv\\;\\operatorname{plim}\\hat{\\tau}_{\\mathrm{dir}}(A^*)-\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}},\\quad\n  \\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}\\;=\\;\\frac{\\operatorname{Cov}(T,f(A))}{\\operatorname{Var}(T)}.\n\\end{equation}\nSubstituting back into~\\eqref{eq:mediation} gives the convex-combination form\n$\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}(C)=\\lambda(C)\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}+(1-\\lambda(C))\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}$, so $\\lambda(C)$ is the weight placing the biased plim between $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ (perfect classification) and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}$ (no information).\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Mediation leakage boundedness]\n\\label{prop:lambda_bound}\nLet $\\lambda(C)$ be defined by~\\eqref{eq:lambda_def} with $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}\\neq 0$, and let $C$ be column-stochastic.\n\\begin{enumerate}\n\\item[(a)] $\\lambda(C)\\leq 1$ iff $\\operatorname{sign}(B(C))=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}})$ (the bias points in the direction of the indirect effect; no overcorrection).\n\\item[(b)] $\\lambda(C)\\geq 0$ iff additionally $|B(C)|\\leq|\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}|$ (the bias does not exceed the indirect effect in magnitude).\n\\end{enumerate}\nEquivalently, $\\lambda(C)\\in[0,1]$ iff $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}(C)\\in[\\min(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}},\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}),\\,\\max(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}},\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})]$.\n\nUnder \\emph{proportional effects} ($f(A)=\\lambda_0\\,g(A)$ with $g(k)=\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A{=}k]$), both (a) and (b) hold for every $C$, so $\\lambda(C)\\in[0,1]$ unconditionally.\nFor \\emph{general (non-proportional)} $f$, both (a) and (b) can fail because $\\operatorname{Cov}(\\mathbb{E}[g\\mid A^*],\\mathbb{E}[f\\mid A^*])$ may differ in sign or magnitude from $\\operatorname{Cov}(g,f)$ (a covariance-level analogue of Simpson's paradox under $A^*$ aggregation); the numerical verification below quantifies the empirical rate.\nThe endpoints $\\lambda(I)=1$ and $\\lambda(C)\\to 0$ in the rank-one (uninformative) limit hold in all regimes.\n\n\\emph{Sign preservation.} When $\\lambda(C)\\in[0,1]$, the convex-combination form gives $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}(C)\\in[\\min(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}},\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}),\\,\\max(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}},\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})]$. If additionally $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$, both endpoints share the same sign, so $\\operatorname{sign}(\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})$ for every~$C$. Under proportional effects, $\\lambda(C)\\in[0,1]$ is automatic (above), so sign preservation of $\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ reduces to the single condition $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\begin{proof}\n\\emph{(a),(b).} With $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}\\neq 0$, $\\lambda(C)\\leq 1\\iff B(C)/\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}\\geq 0\\iff \\operatorname{sign}(B(C))=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}})$. Given (a), $\\lambda(C)\\geq 0\\iff B(C)/\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}\\leq 1\\iff|B(C)|\\leq|\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}|$.\n\n\\emph{Proportional case.} Step~4 of the mediation derivation gives $B(C)=\\lambda_0\\,V_W(C)/(V_W(C)+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)$; the rank-one limit $V_W{\\to}V$ yields $\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}=\\lambda_0\\,V/(V+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)$. Both quantities carry $\\operatorname{sign}(\\lambda_0)$, establishing~(a). The ratio\n\\[\n  \\frac{B(C)}{\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}}\\;=\\;\\frac{V_W(C)\\,(V+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)}{V\\,(V_W(C)+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)}\n\\]\nis monotone increasing in $V_W(C)\\in[0,V]$ with range $[0,1]$ (its derivative in $V_W$ is $\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2(V+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)/[V(V_W+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)^2]>0$), establishing~(b). Hence $\\lambda(C)\\in[0,1]$ unconditionally.\n\n\\emph{General case.} Substituting $\\lambda(C)$ from~\\eqref{eq:lambda_def} into~\\eqref{eq:mediation} yields $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}(C)=\\lambda(C)\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}+(1{-}\\lambda(C))\\,\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}$, so $\\lambda(C)\\in[0,1]$ iff $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}(C)$ lies in the closed interval with endpoints $\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ and $\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}$.\n\n\\emph{Endpoints.} At $C{=}I$, $A^*{=}A$ a.s., $\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*]=g(A)$, and FWL gives $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}=\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$, hence $B(I)=0$ and $\\lambda(I)=1$. At any rank-one $C$ (rows identical), $A^*\\perp A$ so $\\mathbb{E}[T\\mid A^*]$ is constant, the partial regression on $A^*$ has no effect, and $\\operatorname{plim}\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}=\\operatorname{Cov}(Y,T)/\\operatorname{Var}(T)=\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}}$, giving $B=\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ and $\\lambda=0$.\n\\end{proof}\n\n\\paragraph{Relation to the between-variance ratio.}\nA natural \\emph{diagnostic} closely related to $\\lambda(C)$ is the between-variance ratio\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:vb_ratio}\n  \\lambda_V(C) \\;:=\\; \\frac{V_B(C)}{V},\\qquad V_B(C)\\;=\\;\\operatorname{Var}\\!\\bigl(\\mathbb{E}[g(A)\\mid A^*]\\bigr),\\quad V\\;=\\;\\operatorname{Var}(g(A)),\n\\end{equation}\nwhich satisfies $\\lambda_V(C)\\in[0,1]$ \\emph{unconditionally} by the law of total variance applied to $g(A)$. Under proportional effects, $\\lambda$ and $\\lambda_V$ agree at the endpoints and are related by\n\\[\n  1-\\lambda(C)\\;=\\;\\frac{V_W(C)\\,(V+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)}{V\\,(V_W(C)+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2)}\\;=\\;\\bigl(1-\\lambda_V(C)\\bigr)\\cdot\\frac{V+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2}{V_W(C)+\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2},\n\\]\nso they coincide in the limit $\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2\\gg V$. They generally differ for non-proportional $f$. We use $\\lambda$ throughout the main text because it parameterizes~\\eqref{eq:mediation} directly; $\\lambda_V$ remains a useful summary of the channel quality of $A^*$ as a proxy for $A$ (independent of the structural form of $f$).\n\n\\paragraph{Numerical verification.}\nFor each $K\\in\\{3,5,7\\}$ we draw $N{=}10{,}000$ configurations $(\\pi,g,\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2,C)$ with $\\pi\\sim\\operatorname{Dirichlet}(\\mathbf{1})$, $g\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,I_K)$, $\\sigma_\\varepsilon^2\\sim\\operatorname{Uniform}(0.1,2)$, columns of $C\\sim\\operatorname{Dirichlet}(\\mathbf{1})$, and evaluate $\\lambda(C)$ from~\\eqref{eq:lambda_def} in two regimes for $f$:\n\\begin{itemize}\\itemsep0pt\n\\item \\textbf{Proportional} ($f=\\lambda_0\\,g$, $\\lambda_0\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,1)\\!\\setminus\\!\\{0\\}$): $\\lambda(C)\\in[0,1]$ in \\emph{all} $30{,}000$ trials (pooled observed range $[1.2{\\times}10^{-5},\\,0.850]$; zero out-of-bounds), confirming the closed-form bound. Sign preservation of $\\hat\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}}$ therefore reduces to $\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{dir}})=\\operatorname{sign}(\\tau_{\\mathrm{total}})$.\n\\item \\textbf{Non-proportional} ($f\\sim\\mathcal{N}(0,I_K)$ drawn independently of $g$, restricted to $|\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}|\\geq 0.05$ to avoid the ill-conditioned regime where $\\lambda{=}1{-}B/\\tau_{\\mathrm{ind}}$ diverges): $\\lambda(C)$ exceeds $[0,1]$ in $\\sim15$--$17\\%$ of trials, illustrating that (a) and (b) are not automatic outside the proportional case.\n\\end{itemize}\nThe diagnostic $\\lambda_V(C)$ lies in $[0,1]$ across all $30{,}000$ trials irrespective of regime, as guaranteed by the law of total variance. The script and full results are in \\texttt{artifacts/verify\\_lambda\\_bound.py} and \\texttt{artifacts/verify\\_lambda\\_bound\\_results.json}.\n\n\\subsection{Proof of Theorem~\\ref{thm:k3_symmetric} ($K{=}3$ Symmetric Monotonicity)}\n\\label{app:k3_symmetric_proof}\n",
      "numLines": 500,
      "startLine": 1,

    }
  }
